can you sense-check vs. ~/.claude/plans/rosy-watching-hearth.md

> AGENT

Yes. Against [rosy-watching-hearth.md](/Users/user_c042661f/.claude/plans/rosy-watching-hearth.md), these are the main issues I’d tighten:

1. It still doesn’t fully specify how multiple anchors behave in one segment/window.
WGP processes all local `image_refs` together in one generation call, not in sub-batches. So the doc should say explicitly:
- anchors are sparse per-window conditionings
- there is no automatic anchor chunking
- too many anchors is a memory/quality risk, not a handled scaling mode

2. It needs a hard rule for duplicate anchor positions.
Right now, duplicate positions are not meaningfully supported. In WGP staging, later refs overwrite earlier ones at the same absolute slot in the non-custom path, and even in custom injection the semantics are not something we should rely on. The plan should reject duplicate local positions.

3. It should add an anchor-count policy.
The doc currently has no cap or warning threshold. That leaves an important implementation decision open. It should define either:
- a hard per-segment/window max, or
- a soft warning threshold plus “proceed anyway”
Without that, “what if the anchor set is huge?” is unresolved.

4. The alignment invariant needs to be explicit.
If per-anchor strength is added, the doc must treat these as a locked ordered triple:
- `image_refs`
- `frames_positions`
- `image_refs_strengths`
Any mismatch in length or ordering should be a validation error.

5. The anchors-only path is still easy to get wrong.
The current code’s overlap/gating logic is video-centric. If the plan wants anchors-only `ltx_hybrid` to work, it has to explicitly say that anchor presence counts as guidance for segment emission, otherwise the orchestrator will downgrade those segments to `kind=none`.

6. The audio section is mostly right, but should separate two cases more sharply.
- generation-time audio guidance per segment
- final post-stitch mux audio
Those are different systems and should stay separate in the doc.

7. The doc should be more explicit that later-window anchors are “supported in principle, mandatory to test.”
That part is not just a nice-to-have. It is one of the highest-risk behaviors.

So the short version is:
- the plan is directionally right
- it still underspecifies anchor scaling behavior
- it should explicitly define duplicate handling, max-anchor policy, and anchor-only segment gating
- if you add per-anchor strength, the ordered-list contract must be spelled out as a hard invariant

Now do a thorough sense-check on ~/.claude/plans/rosy-watching-hearth.md

> AGENT

Findings:

1. Anchors-only `ltx_hybrid` is still not implementable from this plan as written because the current orchestrator only treats video-backed guidance as overlap-worthy. `_segment_has_travel_guidance_overlap()` checks `videos` and `_guidance_video_url`, not anchors, so an anchors-only segment would be downgraded to `{"kind":"none"}` before it reaches generation. See [orchestrator.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py#L93) and [orchestrator.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py#L1921). The plan needs an explicit anchor-aware gating/remap rule.

2. The plan understates the amount of plumbing needed after `TravelSegmentProcessor`. Today `process_segment()` returns only `video_guide`, `video_mask`, `video_prompt_type`, and `structure_type`, and `StructureOutputs` only carries those fields. `_build_generation_params()` does not consume hybrid-only outputs like `image_refs`, `frames_positions`, `audio_guide`, `audio_prompt_type`, `input_video_strength`, or `audio_scale`. See [segment_processor.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/segment_processor.py#L261), [task_registry.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py#L95), and [task_registry.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py#L620). The plan says “extend return dict,” but not the full downstream struct and builder changes required.

3. `input_video_strength` would currently be dropped on at least one queue path. `db_task_to_generation_task()` whitelists `image_refs`, `frames_positions`, `audio_guide`, and `audio_prompt_type`, but not `input_video_strength`, even though WGP params builders expect it. See [task_conversion.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_conversion.py#L38) and [wgp_params.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/generators/wgp_params.py#L117). So the plan’s “verify passthrough” item should be upgraded to an explicit required code change.

4. The plan is still missing a clear contract split between global hybrid config and segment-local payloads. Current `TravelGuidanceConfig.from_payload()` validates all non-`none` configs by requiring `videos`, which conflicts with the proposed anchors-only or segment-local-anchor payload shapes. See [travel_guidance.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/core/params/travel_guidance.py#L99) and [travel_guidance.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/core/params/travel_guidance.py#L262). The plan needs to state whether:
- one type handles both global and segment-local forms, with different validation modes, or
- there is a separate segment payload contract.

5. The audio section is directionally right but still incomplete. It identifies that segment-level generation audio is new work, but it does not wire `audio.strength` to WGP’s `audio_scale`, and it does not explicitly state where sliced audio files live or how they are cleaned up. Current travel only passes `audio_url` to final stitch. See [orchestrator.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py#L2171). Also, `audio_prompt_type="K"` is valid for distilled LTX, but only when `video_prompt_type` contains `V`, so anchors-only plus control-track audio must be rejected at validation time. See [ltx2_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py#L136) and [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L834).

6. The plan does not fully account for “multiple anchors at once” behavior. WGP processes all local anchors together in one generation call; there is no anchor chunking or batching mechanism. If a segment/window has many anchors, they are all loaded/resized and conditioned together. See [preflight.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/generators/preflight.py#L185), [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L6536), and [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L6654). The doc should define:
- duplicate-position handling
- max or warning threshold for anchors per segment/window
- behavior when too many anchors are supplied

7. Duplicate anchor frame positions are unresolved in the plan. In current WGP staging, duplicate positions are not a cleanly supported semantic; in non-custom injection staging, later refs overwrite earlier refs at the same slot. See [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L6419). The plan should explicitly reject duplicate local positions rather than leaving this implicit.

8. The `VGKFI` / `PVGKFI` / `DVGKFI` / `EVGKFI` assumption is plausible but still the highest-risk runtime assumption. The code strongly suggests mixed letters should work because parsing is presence-based and guide/injected-frame composition already exists. See [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L9010) and [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L6547). But no existing headless path emits these combinations, so the plan is right to call out a focused integration test. That should be a required first implementation milestone, not a mitigation note.

9. The plan’s “how anchors become `image_refs`” section is partly right but slightly misleading. WGP preflight can load `image_refs` from local paths directly, so the headless layer does not need to eagerly decode them into PIL itself. See [preflight.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/generators/preflight.py#L185). The real requirement is to ensure the paths survive through task conversion, filtering, and cleanup.

10. The document still mixes “implementation roadmap” and “sense-check verdict” in a way that leaves a few decisions open. In particular:
- whether anchors-only hybrid is valid in v1
- whether there is a hard cap or soft warning on anchor count
- whether segment payloads carry raw anchor objects or only preflattened `image_refs` / `frames_positions`
Those should be made explicit if this file is meant to be handoff-complete.

What checks out:
- The distinction between LTX-Desktop conceptually and Wan2GP mechanically is correct.
- Shared-strength-only v1 avoids vendored changes and matches current LTX injected-ref behavior in [ltx2.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2.py#L968).
- The doc is correct that `custom_frames_injection` is the key reason later-window anchors are plausible for LTX distilled. See [ltx2_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py#L189) and [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L6422).

Bottom line:
The file is directionally strong, but it is not yet decision-complete. The biggest missing items are:
- anchor-aware segment gating
- downstream generation param plumbing
- explicit anchor scaling/duplicate policy
- validation split between global config and segment-local payloads
- explicit audio-scale mapping and lifecycle details

> AGENT

Findings:

1. The plan still leaves a silent transport gap for anchor images. It proposes `image_refs_paths` as the new `process_segment()` output and says preflight already converts that to `image_refs`, but the only preflight helper that does this today is `prepare_svi_image_refs()`, and it is explicitly SVI-specific. See [preflight.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/generators/preflight.py#L13). The normal generation path in [wgp_params.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/generators/wgp_params.py#L313) expects `image_refs`, not `image_refs_paths`, and there is no generic conversion step shown in the plan. As written, hybrid anchors would likely never become `image_refs` unless you either extend preflight beyond SVI or emit `image_refs` directly into generation params.

2. `image_refs_strengths` is proposed as a new parameter, but the plan never closes the full compatibility loop around WGP parameter filtering. `source.models.wgp.orchestrator` drops any key not present in `wgp.generate_video()`’s signature. See [orchestrator.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py#L913). So adding it only to task conversion and local param builders is insufficient; the vendored `wgp.generate_video()` signature must also accept it, or the key will be sanitized away before the WGP call.

3. The “dual contract” section is still internally inconsistent. It says segment-local `ltx_hybrid` payloads may legally contain neither videos nor anchors, but that collides with the earlier rule that “anchors-only counts as guidance for segment emission.” If a segment has neither local anchors nor local control overlap, there is no reason to emit `ltx_hybrid`; it should be `kind=none`. Allowing empty local hybrid payloads muddies the contract and makes downstream parsing harder. The cleaner rule is: global config may have anchors/control/audio; segment-local payload should be `kind=none` or a non-empty effective local hybrid config.

4. The anchor cap policy is not fully coherent with the actual runtime boundary. The plan sets a soft cap of 4 and hard cap of 8 “per segment/window,” but all segment-local anchors are passed together and later-window filtering is performed inside WGP custom injection. See [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L6422). If you cap only per segment, a long segment may still have more than 8 anchors distributed across windows. If you cap per window, the orchestrator does not currently know the true WGP windowing decomposition at the anchor-filter stage. The plan needs to choose one enforceable policy:
- cap per segment-local payload, or
- cap after explicit WGP-window projection logic in headless code

5. The `VGKFI` “required first step” test is still underspecified for the real risk. The plan says to verify no errors, but that is too weak. The main failure mode is semantic, not just crashing: mixed prompt letters could run while silently dropping either the guide path or the anchor path. The test needs to assert that both conditioning branches are present in the downstream WGP state, not merely that generation completes. Concretely, the test should verify that:
- `video_guide` survives
- `frames_positions` produces non-empty `frames_relative_positions_list`
- `prepare_video_guide_and_mask()` receives injected frames and guide video together
A no-exception test is not enough.

6. The audio section still misses lifecycle and temp-file ownership. It says external audio is downloaded once and sliced per segment, but does not define:
- which component owns cleanup
- whether slices live in orchestrator temp dirs or segment-processing dirs
- whether retries reuse slices or regenerate them
That matters because current travel already has separate orchestrator and segment output dirs, and silent accumulation of sliced audio files will be an operational leak.

7. `control_strength -> denoising_strength` is plausible but not fully justified in the plan. In current LTX control, `denoising_strength` is used when `"G"` is in `video_prompt_type`, and the effective control strength is computed inside the LTX model path. See [ltx2.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2.py#L901). For hybrid modes like `PVGKFI`, `DVGKFI`, etc., that mapping is probably right, but the plan should say explicitly that control strength applies only to the control-video branch and does not affect anchor strengths. Otherwise implementers could accidentally conflate `control_strength` and `input_video_strength`.

8. The plan assumes duplicate positions can be validated both globally and segment-locally, but the global duplicate rule may be too strict if overlap duplication is intentional. For example, if the same source image is intentionally repeated at different segment projections because of overlap handling, global positions are fine, local duplicates are not. The real invariant that matters is no duplicate local positions after remapping within a given emitted segment. Global duplicates only need to be banned if they represent the exact same absolute frame position in the authored journey.

9. The `process_segment()` expansion is directionally right, but the proposed names mix two conventions:
- existing code uses plain WGP-facing names like `video_guide`, `video_prompt_type`
- the plan introduces `image_refs_paths`
That is a smell because it adds one more translation layer. Given the current stack, it would likely be cleaner either to:
- return WGP-facing `image_refs` as already-resolved local paths and then add a generic preflight conversion, or
- standardize all path-carrying image refs on a single `*_paths` convention across the generation stack
As written, the plan picks a one-off name without resolving that broader convention.

10. The plan still understates how much of the current travel path is keyed off `has_structure_guidance`. `_process_structure_guidance()` only runs the shared processor when `travel_mode == "vace"` or `has_structure_guidance` is true. See [task_registry.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py#L567). For `ltx_hybrid`, whether this branch runs depends on how `TravelGuidanceConfig.has_guidance` is redefined. That change is critical and should be called out explicitly; otherwise anchors-only hybrid may not even invoke the processor on some paths.

11. The plan does not mention the existing `TravelGuidanceConfig.needs_ic_lora()` auto-injection site in the orchestrator, even though hybrid pose/depth/canny depends on it. See [orchestrator.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py#L135) and [travel_guidance.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/core/params/travel_guidance.py#L202). It says to update `needs_ic_lora()`, which is correct, but it should explicitly note that hybrid control-only segments, hybrid mixed segments, and anchor-only segments must differ:
- pose/depth/canny + control present => inject IC-LoRA
- raw control only => no IC-LoRA
- anchors only => no IC-LoRA

12. The plan’s “no top-level `anchor_strength`” decision is coherent, but it removes an easy compatibility and ergonomics fallback. If that is intentional, it should state a migration position: callers must specify strength per anchor, with default `1.0`. Otherwise someone implementing UI or API compatibility may reintroduce a shared field ad hoc and create precedence ambiguity.

What does check out:
- The plan correctly identifies the real injected-ref seam in [ltx2.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx2.py#L965).
- It correctly calls out the current anchors-only gating bug in [orchestrator.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py#L93).
- It correctly separates generation-time audio from final mux audio, and it correctly notes the `audio_prompt_type="K"` constraint requiring `V` in `video_prompt_type`. See [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L834).
- It correctly elevates later-window anchor behavior as a high-risk must-test area because of `custom_frames_injection`. See [wgp.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/Wan2GP/wgp.py#L6422).

Bottom line:
The current version is much better than the earlier one, but it is still not fully decision-complete. The biggest unresolved issues are:
- how anchor image paths become `image_refs` outside the SVI-only preflight helper
- full signature/filter plumbing for `image_refs_strengths`
- whether the anchor cap is enforceable per segment or per actual WGP window
- cleanup/ownership for generated audio slices
- tightening the local-vs-global hybrid payload contract so empty local hybrid payloads do not exist

> AGENT

Main issues remaining in [rosy-watching-hearth.md](/Users/user_c042661f/.claude/plans/rosy-watching-hearth.md):

1. The plan still has contradictory rules for what counts as a valid local `ltx_hybrid` payload.
   - In “Implementation Decisions,” it says no empty local hybrid payloads; segments with no local anchors and no control overlap become `kind=none`.
   - In Phase 1c, it says the same validation rule applies globally and locally: `videos OR anchors` must be non-empty.
   - But in Phase 6, it still says “Segment-local validation: empty anchors allowed (they were remapped out).”
   Those three statements cannot all be true. The clean rule should be: segment-local `ltx_hybrid` requires at least one effective local conditioner (`anchors` or `videos`), otherwise emit `kind=none`.

2. `has_guidance` is still internally inconsistent with the validation rules.
   - The plan says `has_guidance` should be `kind != "none" and (has_anchors or has_control or has_audio)`.
   - But the same plan also says `ltx_hybrid` requires at least `videos OR anchors`, and treats generation-time audio as separate from mux audio.
   That means audio-only would count as “guidance” under `has_guidance`, while validation says audio-only is invalid. The plan needs one position. I think the correct one is: audio augments a hybrid segment, but does not by itself make a segment a valid `ltx_hybrid`.

3. There is still a hard transport bug in the whitelist section: `audio_scale` is not already whitelisted.
   - The plan says only `input_video_strength` and `image_refs_strengths` need to be added to the whitelist and that `audio_scale` is already covered.
   - It is not. See [task_conversion.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_conversion.py#L38).
   So if you follow the plan literally, per-segment external audio strength would still get dropped on the DB-task conversion path.

4. The plan still contains a flag contradiction around `_is_segment_local`.
   - In the earlier resolved-decision section, it says no `_is_segment_local` flag is needed.
   - In Phase 1e, it still says `to_segment_payload()` should include `_is_segment_local: true`.
   That should be removed or reinstated consistently. Right now it is undecided in the document.

5. The strength model is cleaner than before, but one stale concept remains: `_default_strength()` for `ltx_hybrid`.
   - The plan says there is no top-level `anchor_strength`.
   - It introduces `control_strength` and per-anchor `strength`.
   - But it also says `_default_strength()` should handle `ltx_hybrid` with defaults like `1.0` or `0.5`.
   That implies a remaining generic shared strength field, which no longer has a clear meaning for hybrid. The doc should either:
   - say `_default_strength()` remains only for legacy kinds, or
   - explicitly define what hybrid-level default it feeds

6. The anchor cap language is improved, but the wording still mixes “per segment/window” with an implementation that only enforces per segment-local payload.
   That is okay as an implementation choice, but it should stop saying “per-window” unless the headless layer actually computes WGP windows. Right now the enforceable rule is per emitted segment payload.

What now looks solid:
- The `image_refs_paths` concern is resolved. `prepare_svi_image_refs()` is badly named, but it is called on the general WGP entrypoint in [orchestrator.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py#L575), so reusing it for hybrid is reasonable.
- The `image_refs_strengths` sanitization issue is correctly captured now: the vendored `wgp.generate_video()` signature must accept it or it will be dropped by [orchestrator.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py#L913).
- The audio lifecycle decision is now explicit enough: per-segment slices in `segment_processing_dir`, regenerated on retry.
- The semantic `VGKFI` gate is much better than before; it now tests both branches instead of just “no crash.”

So the plan is much tighter now. The remaining work is mostly cleanup of internal contradictions, not a fundamental redesign. The highest-signal fixes are:
- make local `ltx_hybrid` validity rules consistent
- remove `has_audio` from `has_guidance` unless audio-only hybrid is actually intended
- add `audio_scale` to the whitelist section
- remove the stale `_is_segment_local` and `_default_strength()` ambiguity

> AGENT

Yes. One more careful pass: the plan is now close, but there are still a few real inconsistencies and one likely implementation trap.

Most important remaining issues:

1. `audio_scale` is still incorrectly described as already whitelisted.
   In the file, the whitelist section says only `input_video_strength` and `image_refs_strengths` need to be added, and that `audio_scale` is already preserved. That is false against current code. [task_conversion.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_conversion.py#L38) does not include `audio_scale`, so generation-time audio strength would still be lost on that path unless the plan explicitly adds it.

2. The plan still contradicts itself on segment-local validity.
   It now correctly says no empty local `ltx_hybrid` payloads, but later still says “Segment-local validation: empty anchors allowed (they were remapped out).” Those are not the same rule. If a segment has no local anchors and no control overlap, it should be `kind=none`, not a valid empty hybrid payload.

3. `has_guidance` still likely overreaches.
   The plan says `has_guidance` should be true when anchors or videos or audio are present. That only makes sense if audio-only `ltx_hybrid` is valid, but elsewhere the same plan defines hybrid as requiring anchors or control. So `has_audio` should not by itself make a segment “guided” unless you intentionally want audio-only hybrid generation.

4. `_default_strength()` for `ltx_hybrid` is still conceptually stale.
   The plan moved to per-anchor `strength` plus `control_strength` and `audio.strength`, which is good. But it still says `_default_strength()` should handle `ltx_hybrid` with `1.0`/`0.5` defaults. That suggests a leftover shared-strength concept that no longer has a clear owner.

5. The anchor cap wording still mixes implementation levels.
   The plan says “soft cap 4 / hard cap 8 per segment/window,” but the actual enforceable implementation it describes is per segment-local payload, not per actual WGP window. That should be stated plainly to avoid implementers overpromising behavior they are not actually calculating.

6. There is one likely transport trap around `image_refs_paths`.
   The plan is now correct that `prepare_svi_image_refs()` is invoked on the general WGP entrypoint and can be reused. But because it is named SVI-specific and only converts `image_refs_paths` when `image_refs` is empty, the implementation must avoid also populating `image_refs` earlier on the same path in a conflicting format. That should be stated as an invariant: hybrid path sets `image_refs_paths`, not `image_refs`, before WGP preflight conversion.

What looks good now:

- The `image_refs_strengths` seam is correctly identified.
- The vendored change scope is small and well-targeted.
- The `VGKFI` semantic gate is now strong enough to be meaningful.
- The audio split between generation-time conditioning and final mux is clearly separated.
- The anchors-only gating bug is properly identified and addressed.
- The alignment invariant is now clearly central, which is right.

Bottom line:
This is now mostly solid. I would not call it fully clean yet because of the `audio_scale` whitelist miss and the remaining contract contradictions around local empty payloads and `has_guidance`. Those should be fixed before treating the file as implementation-complete.

> AGENT

Use this review prompt:

Please do a hostile, fresh-eyes design review of [rosy-watching-hearth.md](/Users/user_c042661f/.claude/plans/rosy-watching-hearth.md) against the actual Headless-Wan2GP codebase.

Your job is not to agree with the plan. Your job is to find:
- hidden contradictions
- missing plumbing
- invalid assumptions
- places where the plan says “works” but current code disagrees
- transport/serialization bugs
- queue/runtime/WGP boundary mismatches
- edge cases that will fail silently
- places where the implementation would be ambiguous for an engineer

Review it from these angles:

1. Contract correctness
- Is the proposed `ltx_hybrid` schema internally consistent?
- Are global vs segment-local payload rules coherent?
- Are validation rules contradictory anywhere?
- Does `has_guidance` match the intended validity rules?

2. Travel orchestrator correctness
- Would anchors-only hybrid actually survive segment overlap/gating?
- Would segments with no local anchors/control be emitted correctly as `kind=none`?
- Is global-to-local anchor remapping fully specified?
- Are overlap-zone anchors handled consistently?

3. Segment processor / task registry plumbing
- If `process_segment()` returns new hybrid fields, do those fields actually reach generation?
- Are `StructureOutputs`, `_process_structure_guidance()`, and `_build_generation_params()` fully covered?
- Is there any place where values would be silently dropped?

4. Queue / DB task conversion / worker transport
- Are all new params whitelisted and preserved?
- Would any field be lost during DB-task conversion, queue serialization, or worker dispatch?
- Check especially:
  - `image_refs_paths`
  - `frames_positions`
  - `image_refs_strengths`
  - `input_video_strength`
  - `audio_guide`
  - `audio_prompt_type`
  - `audio_scale`
  - `denoising_strength`

5. WGP boundary correctness
- Would `_filter_wgp_params()` drop anything unless vendored signatures are updated?
- Is the plan clear about every vendored signature that must change?
- Does `image_refs_paths` really become `image_refs` on the non-SVI path, or is that assumption shaky?
- Is the `image_refs` / `frames_positions` / `image_refs_strengths` alignment preserved end to end?

6. Wan2GP / LTX behavior correctness
- Is the proposed per-anchor strength change actually minimal and backward compatible?
- Are there any other places in `ltx2.py` where shared `input_video_strength` semantics would conflict?
- Does mixed prompt type composition (`VGKFI`, `PVGKFI`, etc.) look genuinely valid, or just plausible?
- Are there any hidden assumptions in WGP letter parsing or preprocessing that the plan misses?

7. Audio model correctness
- Is generation-time audio wiring distinct from final mux audio everywhere?
- Is `audio_prompt_type="K"` used correctly and with all constraints?
- Is `audio_scale` mapped correctly?
- Is segment-level audio slicing actually sufficient, or are there timing/cleanup/retry gaps?

8. Operational / quality risks
- Does the anchor cap policy make sense at the level it is enforced?
- Are duplicate anchor positions handled correctly?
- Are there silent failure modes if too many anchors are supplied?
- Are temp files, downloads, and retries handled cleanly?

9. Test adequacy
- Are the proposed tests sufficient to prove the design?
- Which proposed tests are too weak?
- What high-risk behavior is still untested?
- What should be tested first before implementation proceeds?

Please return:
- Findings first, ordered by severity
- Each finding with file/line references where possible
- Then a short section called `Open Questions`
- Then a short section called `What Looks Sound`

Also explicitly answer:
- “If an engineer implemented this plan literally, where would it most likely break first?”
- “What is the most likely silent bug?”
- “What single assumption in the plan is riskiest?”

If you want, I can also turn that into a shorter “drop into Claude/ChatGPT” version and a stricter “senior engineer code review” version.
